```text
213	\begin{table}[h]
214	\centering
215	\caption{Extended downstream benchmarks (LLaMA-3.1-8B). HellaSwag: 10-shot normalized acc; PIQA: 0-shot normalized acc. KL 30\% uniform: 3-seed mean$\pm$std; others: seed 42.}
216	\label{tab:downstream}
217	\small
218	\begin{tabular}{llcc}
219	\toprule
220	Sparsity & Method & HellaSwag & PIQA \\
221	\midrule
222	0\% & Dense & \textbf{82.35} & \textbf{80.90} \\
223	\midrule
224	\multirow{3}{*}{30\%}
225	& KL uniform & \textbf{71.07$\pm$0.54} & \textbf{74.95$\pm$0.25} \\ % exp_id: hellaswag_piqa_kl30_uniform (3-seed)
226	& TEAL uniform & 63.31 & 72.80 \\ % exp_id: bench_teal30_uniform_6task
227	& BCE isocompute & 62.81 & 74.21 \\ % exp_id: bce_isocompute_30u_hellaswag_piqa
228	\midrule
229	\multirow{3}{*}{50\%}
230	& KL global & \textbf{58.95} & \textbf{69.48} \\ % exp_id: hellaswag_piqa_kl50_global
231	& KL uniform & 56.66 & 69.10 \\ % exp_id: hellaswag_piqa_kl50_uniform
232	& TEAL uniform & 39.35 & 63.66 \\ % exp_id: bench_teal50_uniform_6task
233	\bottomrule
234	\end{tabular}
235	\end{table}
236	
237	At 30\%, the KL predictor retains 86.3\% of dense HellaSwag accuracy (71.07$\pm$0.54 vs.\ 82.35, 3-seed) and 92.6\% of PIQA (74.95$\pm$0.25 vs.\ 80.90), confirming moderate degradation on non-reasoning tasks.
238	At 50\%, global allocation outperforms uniform on HellaSwag (+2.29pp), consistent with the budget-concentration benefit observed for PPL in the capacity-limited regime (\S\ref{sec:scaling}); PIQA shows a smaller gap (+0.38pp).
239	
240	
241	\section{Compensation Training Convergence}
242	\label{app:comp_convergence}
```

```text
112	At 50\%, C1 matches KL within statistical noise (max $\Delta$$\leq$0.79pp, s42), while magnitude-trained BCE yields PPL 28.13$\pm$0.20 ($2.4\times$ worse; Table~\ref{tab:main}).
113	At 30\%, all three C1 predictors reproduce KL quality within $\pm$1.70pp across downstream tasks (max $\Delta$=1.70pp MMLU, s42); retrained seeds (s123, s456) show tighter agreement ($\leq$1.18pp).
114	These results confirm that KL's coupled gradients discover mask targets that yield lower perplexity and transfer to a simpler objective (BCE): mask quality is the proximate driver of inference-stage quality, while gradient coupling remains necessary at the discovery stage. % exp_id: bce50_3seed, c1_bce_kl_targets_30u, c1_s123
115	
116	\paragraph{Soft-vs-Hard Confound.}
117	C1 changes both gradient coupling and mask representation (hard binary vs.\ soft Gumbel-Sigmoid). However, BCE with Gumbel-Sigmoid relaxation (even without penalty) yields PPL 32.22 (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), worse than standard hard-target BCE (27.97): softness alone does not explain KL's advantage. % exp_id: bce_gumbel_no_penalty_s42, eval_bce_v3_s42
118	Disentangling target quality from gradient representation remains for future work.
119	
120	\subsection{Ablation Studies}
121	
```

```json
{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "0ab76f82-f4db-4f0a-abb6-88cd1fd6c9c0",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "377b34f0-b0a7-4e84-b4e3-0791dd396766",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "d6545448-91a3-4988-a87d-17fdc89c9910",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_14/projects/distill_sparse_swiglu/paper/introduction.tex",
    "oldString": "(51\\% over TEAL)",
    "newString": "(50\\% over TEAL)",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\nSwiGLU feed-forward networks~\\cite{shazeer2020glu} in modern LLMs exhibit high contextual sparsity: at any given token, a large fraction of FFN neurons contribute negligibly to the output.\nLearned sparsity predictors~\\cite{liu2023dejavu,gautam2026fastforward} exploit this property by training small networks to anticipate which neurons are important, but they universally rely on binary cross-entropy (BCE) loss, treating each neuron as an independent classification target.\nYet at inference, sparsity budgets are allocated globally: the TEAL (training-free activation sparsity;~\\citealp{liu2025teal}) top-$k$ mechanism ranks all neurons across all layers and retains only those above a model-wide threshold.\nThis mismatch between per-neuron training and global inference allocation has not been formally analyzed.\n\nGradient analysis reveals a structural asymmetry: BCE's gradient $\\partial \\mathcal{L}_{\\text{BCE}} / \\partial s_j = \\sigma(s_j) - m_j^*$ depends only on neuron $j$, while under KL divergence, the gradient couples each neuron to all others through the downstream Jacobian (\\S\\ref{sec:method:gradient}).\nKL's coupled gradients discover neuron importance patterns that diverge 68\\% from magnitude-based masks (per-layer Jaccard 32\\%).\nA target-swap ablation confirms that these discovered mask targets are the proximate quality driver at inference: BCE trained on KL-derived targets reproduces KL quality (max $\\Delta$$\\leq$0.79pp at 50\\%, s42), while magnitude-trained BCE achieves 28.13$\\pm$0.20. Gradient coupling remains necessary at the discovery stage.\n\nWe instantiate a lightweight predictor (75.9M params, $<$1\\% of base) trained with forward KL and Gumbel-Sigmoid relaxation~\\cite{jang2017gumbel} on LLaMA-3.1-8B~\\cite{grattafiori2024llama3}.\nAt 30\\% sparsity under uniform allocation, the predictor reduces TEAL's perplexity by 19\\% (8.81 vs.\\ 10.82) with stable downstream (MMLU 40.7$\\pm$1.3\\%, 3-seed); global top-$k$ amplifies cross-seed variance ($7.5\\times$).\nAt 50\\%, the perplexity advantage widens (51\\% over TEAL), but downstream reasoning collapses regardless of method.\n\nOn Mistral-7B-v0.3~\\cite{jiang2023mistral}, KL outperforms TEAL on 5 of 6 downstream tasks at 30\\% (ARC +9.0pp, HellaSwag +8.8pp, WG +5.6pp; GSM8K reverses); on Qwen-2.5-14B~\\cite{qwen2024qwen25}, PPL generalizes but reasoning declines sharply (GSM8K $-$25pp) and MMLU reverses (63.7\\% vs.\\ TEAL's 71.8\\%), suggesting task-and-scale-dependent effects.\nOur contribution is empirical: we characterize how the training objective shapes sparsity prediction quality, and analyze interactions between allocation strategy, sparsity level, and model scale. Wall-clock speedup requires sparse kernel support not addressed here (Appendix~\\ref{app:latency}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item A target-swap ablation confirms that KL's discovered mask targets are the proximate inference-stage quality driver; gradient coupling remains necessary at the discovery stage (\\S\\ref{sec:mask_analysis}).\n\\item These mask patterns render post-hoc compensation (+67.2M params) redundant at both tested sparsity levels (\\S\\ref{sec:isocompute}).\n\\item Analysis of allocation-to-downstream interactions at 7--14B: non-monotonic allocation preference at 8B and scale-dependent effects at 14B (\\S\\ref{sec:scaling}).\n\\end{itemize}\n",
    "structuredPatch": [

        "oldLines": 7,

          " At 30\\% sparsity under uniform allocation, the predictor reduces TEAL's perplexity by 19\\% (8.81 vs.\\ 10.82) with stable downstream (MMLU 40.7$\\pm$1.3\\%, 3-seed); global top-$k$ amplifies cross-seed variance ($7.5\\times$).",
          "-At 50\\%, the perplexity advantage widens (51\\% over TEAL), but downstream reasoning collapses regardless of method.",

          " ",

          " Our contribution is empirical: we characterize how the training objective shapes sparsity prediction quality, and analyze interactions between allocation strategy, sparsity level, and model scale. Wall-clock speedup requires sparse kernel support not addressed here (Appendix~\\ref{app:latency})."
```
